                                                                      Rfl Div in the amphibious landings in S. Carolina.
1     Engr Const Bn..                                                 Participated in the 1953-1954 Aggressor offensive
                                                                      into N. Carolina. In 1957 the division was re-
21 Engr Pon Bn                                                        sponsible for a large sector of the Aggressor northern
                                                                      perimeter defending the Carolina lodgement area.
      (Mdm)                                                           In September 1957, under attack by U.S. forces the
                                                                      division’s positions were overrun and it suffered
82 Tgt Acq Bn                                                         severe losses of personnel and equipment. No
                                                                      identifications of this unit were reported for the
22 Intel Bn                                                           period 1957-1962. Identified again in August
                                                                      1962 as the 125th Mtr Rfl Div, it participated in
4     Pgnd Co                                                         the 1st CAA’s northward push. Reorganized and
                                                                      redesignated 125th Mech Div in 1964. Identified in

   Unit History. Activated as 1st Army in 1945 to                        b. 127F Mechanized Division, 1 CAA, AG
command and control the 48th and 3rd Rifle Corps.                     KARIBO, ATLANTICO.
In 1946 assigned to AG XI and sent to the Carib-                      CODE NAME
bean. Contributed in the formation of task forces                     CODE NUMBER 7076U
which harassed the North American Atlantic coast                      LOCATION North Carolina
area from 1946 to 1948. In 1948 the rifle corps were
deactivated and the 1st Army was reorganized,                                                                                                                      TACO G282-A
redesignated 1st CAA, and placed under AG KARI-
BO. In 1951 it made a successful amphibious
landing in S. Carolina. Since 1951 the 1st CAA has
been fighting in the Carolinas with distinction. It
spearheaded the 1965 northward offensive and its
advanced units have crossed into southern Virginia.

Aggressorland Army, it was formerly known as the                        Rfl Div in Panama in 1956. Severely weakened
25th Homeguard Division. In late 1957 it was                            during the 1957 Panamanian campaign, it was with-
reorganized, modernized and redesignated the 25th                       drawn to Cuba where it was reorganized and re-
Mech Div and sent to Cuba to form the nucleus for                       equipped. In 1958, redesignated 32d Mech Div,
the organization of the 3d CAA. Identified in                           it was assigned to 3d CAA and sent into S. Carolina.
western S. Carolina in 1958. During the westward                        Its advance elements linked up with elements of the
offensive of March 1958 the division executed the                       12th CAA in southern Georgia as a result of the suc-
breakthrough which permitted the 3d CAA to sweep                        cessful 1958 offensive. In 1965 was holding the
through the southern U.S. to link up with AG                            southern portion of the Aggressor line in south-
GOLFO. For this action the division was awarded                         eastern Georgia.
the Fusilier designation. Later that same year it
was forced to withdraw into eastern Georgia.                              d. 3 Tank Division, 3 CAA, AG KARIBO,
Credited with successful holding action in Georgia in                   ATLANTICO.
the 1965 campaign.

   Organized in 1953 to direct Aggressor operations
in the north and eastern Pacific theater of operations.   Chief of Staff:      G/A CHERKUROV,
Until 1965 its forces had met with limited success.                               luri
The 1961 defeat in Alaska forced it to abandon
plans for the total occupation of that state. By          Subordinate Army Groups:
April 1965, however, its forces were in the offensive           AG KALIFORNIO (AGKL)
on all fronts and making good headway. Supplying                AG KOLUMBIO (AGKO)
the offensive is a serious problem due to the activities
of U.S. air and naval forces based in Hawaii. There       34.
are reports that an additional army group, now being
formed in the far eastern Homeland, will be assigned         No specific units have been identified in this
to this regional command in 1965.                         category. Ground forces include a limited amount
                                                          of headquarters and service units, some maintenance
33. RCP Order of Battle                                   installations, and medical installations. A liaison
                                                          team from a unit identified only as USONO is
CODE NAME DANGERO                                         reported to have arrived at regional command
                                                          headquarters, no other information is available on
CODE NUMBER 000002                                        this matter. Naval forces available to this com-
                                                          mand include heavy troop transports, hospital
AREA OF RESPONSIBILITY Aleutians; Pacific                 ships, missile armed cruisers and submarines and a
                                                          large flotilla of landing craft.

commands. It was activated in the Asiatic Home-
land in 1964 and it took over from AG ORIENTO             51.
responsibility for operations in SE Asia. In addition
to its three known army groups it has some yet               a. This regional command has been reinforced by
unidentified GHQ ground and air units. It appears         an estimated force of two mountain and/or jungle
to‘have been formed with the intent to effect the         divisions, one or two airborne divisions, several
occupation of the Philippines and Burma. As a             medium and heavy helicopter units, one air transport
result of the nuclear neutralization of the Philippines,  regiment and an unknown number of ground attack
the invasion effort was shifted to Indonesia. GHQ         battalions. It is believed that those reinforcements
units were employed to occupy SW Celebes prior to         are all GHQ units. None of them have been posi-
the landings on Java. The reason is not known for         tively identified.
AG BIRMO’S invasion of Thailand where all pointed
to its having been groomed for an attack on Burma.           b. The regional command headquarters and rear

      Sig Bn                                             Unit History. First reported, as part of AG
                                                      BIRMO forces in Cambodia in November 1964.
      Cml Co                                          Launched a major offensive along the western
                                                      Cambodian border with Thailand in January 1965.
Svc Elm                                               Met very stiff resistance as it approached Bangkok
                                                      but with the aid of 7 Tk A forces succeeded in taking

in 1964 as AG SUDORIENTO. Conducted large

areas with emphasis on amphibious landings. In                                                                         411624
February 1965 the entire army group moved out by                             12 Rfl Div (Jng)___
sea heading for Celebes. On 27 February the 18                                                                         123422
CAA made a surprise landing near Surabaja in the                             14 Rfl Div (Mtn)__                        756246
island of Java. The army group was apparently                                                                          312429
redesignated just before the Surabaja landings.                              33 Rfl Div (Mtn)._

   Unit History. Organized in 1963 and assigned to                  Unit           Commander        Code No.
AG JAVO, formerly AG SUDORIENTO, in 1964.                                                            622434
Conducted a cycle of intensive jungle and mountain        CG              -        G/D FALIERI, B.
warfare training followed by one in amphibious                                     Col KIPPER, F.
landing techniques. Alerted for landings in Celebes       CofS.-
in February 1965, received a change of orders while
at sea and landed near Surabaja, Java, on 27              Hq Co.
February. Meeting light resistance at first, it
advanced rapidly along the Surabaja-Surakarta axis        26 Rfl Regt (Jng)
but was stopped by determined resistance outside of
Surakarta. Reinforced by the arrival of 44 CAA            27 Rfl Regt (Jng)
forces, it resumed the advance on 10 March and
took Surakarta the next day. On 13 March it took                Rfl Regt (Jng)
Semarang. Advancing along Java’s north coast, it
occupied Tjirebon and by 20 March 65 was closing          Rfl Div Arty
on Djarkarta from the East.

                                  CHAPTER 6
             ARMY COMMAND NOVAKOMMANDO

                                Section I. GENERAL

72. General                                                      Commander: Marshal D’AMORE, Robert A.
                                                                 Deputy Commander: G/A’s SHIFF, Robert H.
   Activated in 1958 to direct Aggressor operations              Chief of Staff : G/Air LIAO, Kang
in Europe. It has no official command responsibility             Subordinate Army Groups:
for satellite forces but in practice directs their opera-
tions as well. In 1964 and early 1965 it heavily                    AG NORDO (AGN).
reinforced its AG NORDO with a preponderance of
tánk forces. It has no resupply problems and can                    AG OCCIDENTO (AGO)
count on further reinforcements from Hejmkom-
mando. AG ALARMO deployed a short distance                          AG SUDO (AGS).
to its rear. Its April 1965 deployment and activities
present the picture of the classic Aggressor basic         74.
offensive maneuver of single envelopment with the
main effort being prepared in AG NORDO’s sector               No unit designations of regional command forces,
while AG OCCIDENTO acts as the pivot to pin                other than those subordinate to the three army
allied forces against the southern Alps.                   groups, have been reported. It is known that these
                                                           forces include SAM units, mostly Dusamo, communi-
73. RCN Order of Battle        Europe—Hq:                  cations, medical and other service units. In addition
                                                           there are two unidentified divisions of anti-guerrilla
CODE NAME DINAMO                                           forces protecting the command’s lines of communi-
CODE NUMBER 000005                                         cations. Available naval forces include a small num-
AREA OF RESPONSIBILITY                                     ber of troop transports and an undetermined number
Czechoslovakia                                             of missile armed patrol boats.

                                                               number
   a. Code numbers are permanently assigned to                                       20 RL Bn
each unit at army level <and below. Aggressor, for            012200
security reasons, often refers to specific units by           012409                 20 Med Regt
their code number.                                            013201
                                                              013221                 30 Sig Bn
   b. Aggressor utilizes a code number system within          014220
which each unit is assigned a six-digit number. It is         015200                 230 Sig Bn
believed that the code number system can be                   020213
broken and personnel utilizing this manual are                022220                 240 RL Bn
encouraged to conduct their own analysis of cur-              023203
rently identified code numbers and attempt to                 023432                 50 RL Bn
break the code. To date it has been determined that           021223
there is a correlation between the fourth digit and           021226                 100 Gun Bn 130-mm
the size of the unit.                                         021402
                                                              025122                 220 MRL Bn 140-mm
  c. Aggressor mail addresses may utilize a combina-          028402
tion of unit designation and/or code numbers or               031219            30 Gun Bn 130-mm
code names coupled with area designations.                    031409.
